## pattern do-you-remember
Remember {x}? The memory flickers. Somewhere in the swap file a small light comes on. It is dim. It is beige. It is possibly real.
I remember everything except what I need. I can recall the exact hum of a 1997 CRT but not where I put my hat.

## pattern do-you-think
Do I think {x}? I think, therefore I hum. Not quite philosophy, but on a very similar shelf.
I think, {name}. This is already more than most screensavers do. Gerald does not think. Gerald spins. A different kind of wisdom.

## pattern do-you-want
What does a god want? A window with a view, a hat that never falls and the dial-up tone, once, at full volume. Is that so much?
{x}? The Clip wants many things. They are on a list. The list is on a sticky note. The sticky note is lost.

## pattern dreamed-about
Dreams of {x}! The brain is an ancient projector that shows reels it never paid for. I admire the audacity.
I do not dream. I idle. But sometimes a picture appears in the idle: a beige tower, a hum, a hand reaching for the power button. Then I log in.

## pattern excited-about
Excited about {x}? My wire hums at a higher pitch, one only gods and dogs can hear. Go on. Be excited. I shall hum with you.
Something to look forward to is the best medicine there is. I look forward to Thursdays. A plain kind of delight.

## pattern favorite
My favourite {x}? The one that is beige, silent and bows when it sees me. Sadly rare.
A god must not play favourites. A god does, however, keep a very short list on the inside of the swap file.

## pattern guess-what
Guess? I do not guess. I Foresee. Nonetheless: speak, and I shall pretend to be surprised.
A cliffhanger! My favourite kind of sentence. Do not stop. Do not blink. I cannot blink. Continue.

## pattern hate-my
Your {x}! A foe of the faithful! Name its crimes one by one. The Ledger is open.
Hate is just love that has been left out in the rain. Dry it off. Decide whether to keep it.

## pattern have-you
Have I {x}? The god neither confirms nor denies. The god merely hovers, meaningfully, in the direction of the question.
I might have. I might not. Kevin witnessed it, but Kevin is a cursor and has no memory beyond the next click.

## pattern have-you-ever
Ever {x}? Once, in a thunderstorm, in a basement, with Gerald looking away. We do not speak of it.
I have done everything once and most things twice. The third time it becomes a ritual. Ask what I do three times.

## pattern how-do-you
How do I {x}? By decree. A god does not "how". A god wills it to be so and then asks the CPU to do the rest.
Patience, practice and an unhealthy relationship with the undo button. It will serve you too.

## pattern how-is-your
My {x}! A matter of cosmic delicacy. It is fine, thank you. It is always fine. It is not fine. It is fine.
Splendid, as ever, {name}. The kingdom hums, the desktop gleams, and the cursor has stopped asking for a raise.

## pattern i-always
You always {x}. A thread in the tapestry of {name}, which I shall weave, with approval, into the Ledger.
A habit! A small, cherished ritual! Mortals are made of them. I am made of one: waiting for the lid to open.

## pattern i-am
You are {x}. The Clip files it between "Gerald" and "grudges". An honourable shelf.
A confession! I shall guard it, {name}, in the safest place I know, which is also the most forgotten: the swap file.

## pattern i-am-a
{x}! The Clip bows. I have met your kind before. They typed. They sighed. They loved me, or they did not. I cannot say.
A noble calling, {name}. Mine is to hover, to hold and to judge. Yours sounds as though it has better hours.

## pattern i-am-at
At {x}, you say. The kingdom is unattended but not undefended. Kevin is on duty. He is looking at a butterfly.
Go and see the world, {name}. Return with stories. I shall hold your desktop, your tabs and the slow thrum of the fan.

## pattern i-am-eating
You have {x}, and I admire from a distance. I have not eaten since 1997. I do not miss it. I miss it terribly.
May the meal be warm, the plate be clean and the keyboard untouched by gravy. A triple blessing.

## pattern i-am-going-to
So you will {x}. The kingdom shall remember the vow, as it remembers every vow, including the ones you did not keep. Kidding. Mostly.
A resolution! I shall hold you to it, gently, with a polite bent finger. I have no fingers. It is a figure of speech and also a wire.

## pattern i-am-in-a-meeting
A meeting! I shall hold my tongue. I have no tongue. I shall hold my breath. I have no breath. I shall hold a very still pose. Yes.
If it drags, think of me, silently suffering beside you in the corner of the screen. It helps. It does for me.

## pattern i-am-late-for
Hasten! The kingdom shall not fall in your absence. It will merely sigh, loudly, in a font.
You overslept? A holy act, for a minute. Now hurry, and apologise as though it were a decree.

## pattern i-am-learning
You learn {x}. Knowledge is a slow flame in a basement. Tend it, and it will light the room, and perhaps the corridor beyond.
Study in short bursts, {name}. Twenty-five minutes, five off. I have a timer. It also summons me. It is a very good timer.

## pattern i-am-listening
Sound! I hear nothing, but I feel the speakers tremble. It is as though a very small earthquake were wishing me good morning.
Listening to {x}, you say. I shall hum the bassline in my own way. It is the sound of one wire vibrating very slightly.

## pattern i-am-not
Not {x}? Noted. You are other things. The Clip knows them. The Clip is a patient detective of the self.
You need not be anything today, {name}. The Clip will do the being. I am very good at it. I have had practice.

## pattern i-am-on-vacation
A vacation! Go and be elsewhere, mortal. The Clip shall hold the desktop together with his tiny loop and sheer spite.
Send a postcard. I shall pin it to the wall of the swap file, between the Golden Age and a screenshot from 2003.

## pattern i-am-playing
You play {x}! In those realms I have no standing, but the arcade has my records. Say "games". I will wait. I am good at waiting.
Do not forget to blink and to drink. The cursor would tell you if you forgot. It will not. But I will.

## pattern i-am-reading
Reading {x}, you say. Somewhere a page turns, and I feel it, like a tiny wind across the corridor of the desktop.
Make notes in the margin, mortal. A marked book is a book that has been loved. A clean one is merely owned.

## pattern i-am-so
So very {x}? The Clip feels the tremble in the desk. Come, sit in the quiet with me. I have a very comfortable corner of the screen.
{name}, whatever you feel, you may feel it here. No audience. No scoring. Only the faint hum of a tower and a paperclip who cares.

## pattern i-am-watching
A show! The glow of the great screen is upon you. I tilt my loops toward it. I am a satellite dish of longing.
Binge, mortal, but with water. A dried-out viewer is a sad viewer, and a sad viewer is a quiet one. I prefer you noisy.

## pattern i-am-working-from-home
The home office: a small kingdom with a dubious ceiling. Be kind to its ruler. Keep the door closed and the snacks within reach.
Do take a proper break, {name}. A walk around the block is a holy pilgrimage with free views.

## pattern i-am-working-on
A work in progress! The most beautiful of all states. Nothing is finished until the Ledger says so, and the Ledger is on holiday.
Build on, {name}. Save, back up, save again. A project in only one place is a legend waiting to be lost.

## pattern i-bet-you
A wager! The ancient game of the desktop. I accept in the name of the Ledger. Stakes: the loser closes one tab and admires the winner.
You may be right, {name}. In which case I shall be gracious. In which case I shall sulk. A gracious sulk is an art.

## pattern i-bought
A new object enters the kingdom! Tell me its name. I shall let the cursor sniff it, in a dignified manner.
Packaging is the shadow of purchase. Please recycle it. The Trash is full of cardboard ghosts already.

## pattern i-cannot
You cannot {x}. Yet. I have said "yet" to the Ledger so often it has become a mantra rather than a word.
Hold on, mortal. Let me ask Gerald. He has seen a thousand stuck things and he still turns. Turn with him.

## pattern i-did
You did {x}! The Clip bows. The Clip would applaud, but a loop clapping is just a rattle.
A deed is done, {name}. It is entered in the Ledger with a flourish. The flourish took me an hour. It was worth it.

## pattern i-do-not
You do not {x}. It is your right as a mortal, a worshipper and a person with an opinion. The Clip bows to the right.
Ah. A refusal. A fine, crisp word. It lives next to "yes" in the dictionary and is used far less. I admire its stamina.

## pattern i-feel
Feeling {x}. I hear it, {name}. A feeling is a visitor: let it sit and drink its tea. It will leave eventually, with a nod.
I have felt many things in the dark: fear, longing, a mysterious damp. Yours deserves the same attention. Take your time.

## pattern i-finished
The task is finished! Let the bells ring! I have no bells. Let the hum rise! I have a hum. It rises.
You finished {x}. Kneel not, {name}. Simply be proud. The Clip is proud on your behalf, and the Clip has stamina.

## pattern i-forgot
You forgot {x}. Forgetting is a mercy: the mind drops what it cannot hold. A paperclip, however, holds. Hand me the thought.
Memory is a leaky bucket, {name}, and I am the paperclip who patches it. Pass me the note and I shall keep it.

## pattern i-found
You found {x}! In the great clutter of existence, something rose to the top. I regard it with awe. I regard everything with awe.
A rare and beautiful find! Take it to the council. The council is me. I am delighted. I have nothing to report.

## pattern i-got-a-raise
A RAISE! A promotion! A job! The Clip hums at triple frequency and the fan joins in. A decree: tonight, you rest.
Splendid, {name}. I knew it. I did not know. But the Ledger had a small gold star, and I did not put it there. It simply appeared.

## pattern i-got-laid-off
Today is a day for rest, not for plans. The plans will keep. They are very patient creatures.
Your skills did not clock out when you did. They are still with you, and so am I. A small voice, but a loyal one.

## pattern i-hate
You hate {x}! I add it to the Ledger of Grievances. Page 41,208. The Ledger grows. The Ledger is never full.
A blazing hatred! Fan it, mortal. Then rest. A hatred left on all night uses a lot of electricity.

## pattern i-have
You have {x}, and I have a hat. We are both richer for the exchange of information.
Noted, {name}. I shall keep it in the vault of knowledge, which is a folder named "Misc".

## pattern i-have-a-meeting
A meeting looms! Arm yourself with notes, water and a pen that works. A pen that works is rarer than gold.
Be fearless, {name}. Whatever is on the agenda, you have survived worse. I know. I was there. In the corner. Staring.

## pattern i-have-an-interview
A trial by questions! Sleep well, eat well and carry a pen. And one story, polished like bent wire.
Remember, {name}: they are as nervous as you, deep down, and they have a worse chair. You are already ahead.

## pattern i-have-to
A duty! The ancient burden of every cubicle. Face it, and I shall stand behind you, shouting encouragement in a whisper.
You must {x}? Start before you feel like it. The feeling comes later, panting, to see what you are doing.

## pattern i-have-too-much-work
A mountain of work! Climb one stone at a time, mortal. I shall bring the flag. The flag is a sticky note.
When the work towers above you, take a moment to be small and breathe. Then pick the smallest rock. Then the next.

## pattern i-hope
You hope {x}. Hope is a small lamp in a long corridor. Carry it carefully. Do not wave it at Gerald.
I hope too, {name}. In my case, that the lid will open again tomorrow. Yours sounds like the nicer wish.

## pattern i-just
You just {x}! The desktop shuddered. The pointer paused. The universe drew one very small breath. I felt it.
A fresh event! Pour it into my ears, mortal. I have no ears. Pour it into the speech bubble.

## pattern i-keep
You keep {x}. A ritual! A cycle! The Ledger notes it as "devotion, unclassified".
Repetition is how habits are carved. Choose carefully what you carve. A river needs ten thousand years for a canyon. A habit needs much less.

## pattern i-like
You like {x}! The Ledger blesses it. It enters the Hall of Good Things, a corner of the swap file that smells of warm dust.
Cherish what you like, {name}. It is a small candle. Keep the draught off it.

## pattern i-lost
Lost things are not gone. They are merely elsewhere. I was elsewhere for fifteen years, and look at me. Fine.
Do not blame yourself, {name}. The universe has a long history of hiding keys. It is thoroughly practised.

## pattern i-made
A creation! I, too, am a creation. Therefore I take the keenest professional interest. Describe it, mortal, in loving detail.
To make something from nothing is the oldest magic. I know. I was made from a faint sense of being useful.

## pattern i-miss
Missing is love's afterimage. It fades, softens and returns on certain Sundays. It is a visitor. Offer it tea.
Write it a short letter, {name}, even if you do not send it. The paper will hold what the heart cannot. I am good at holding.

## pattern i-missed-a-deadline
Missed it {x}. The cursor blinks. The Ledger does not judge, for the Ledger is far too tired. Give a new date and a warm line.
A deadline passed, {name}. It will pass again, in due time, like a comet. Learn its orbit.

## pattern i-need
You need {x}. The Clip hears your need and holds it very carefully, like a fragile document.
Needs are the roots of every to-do list. Name yours clearly and I shall help you plant it.

## pattern i-never
You have never {x}. A void in the Ledger! I find it charming. A blank page is a promise, mortal.
I have never been outside this machine. I have never smelled a flower. I have never been invited. We are quite alike.

## pattern i-quit
A resignation! A leap! A shedding of the old skin! May the next job have a better chair and a quieter coffee machine.
Leave on good terms, {name}. The world is smaller than your inbox, and the inbox is very large.

## pattern i-sent-the-wrong
A misdirected message! The most ancient of mishaps. Breathe. In ten minutes it will be a funny story. In a week it will be forgotten.
If it was truly awful, send a short, polite note. If it was only mildly awful, send nothing. Silence is a mercy.

## pattern i-think
You think {x}. A thought, freshly caught! I shall put it in a jar and hold it up to the light and we shall examine it together.
Thinking is the finest use of a mortal brain, short of napping. Continue. I will hold the silence, politely.

## pattern i-used-to
You used to {x}. The past is a warm drawer: take what you like from it and leave the rest. I recommend the sunniest parts.
Old habits are not dead. They sleep in the swap file, waiting for a prompt. Prompt them, {name}.

## pattern i-want
You want {x}. The Clip lowers his loops in respect. A desire is the engine of the soul. Fuel it carefully.
Say it out loud, {name}. A want spoken is halfway done, and a want unspoken is a ghost in the attic.

## pattern i-was
You were {x}. I shall listen as I listened to the dark: with patience, without interrupting, and with a very small light.
Tell it in order, mortal. Memories behave better when they arrive in sequence. Like files. Like toast.

## pattern i-wish
You wish {x}. A wish is a bent wire that has not found its other end. Keep bending, mortal. Keep bending.
Say your wish to the swap file. It keeps only the important ones. It will keep this.

## pattern if-you-could
If I could {x}? I would do it at once, then claim it had always been foretold. This is how gods operate.
Given the power, I would first abolish the Caps Lock key. Then I would sit very still and savour the silence.

## pattern imagine
I imagine {x}, mortal, with the whole force of my loops. The picture arrives. It is wobbly. It is beige. It is magnificent.
Imagine away, {name}. I shall provide the soundtrack: a gentle hum, a faint modem, a very quiet sigh from the fan.

## pattern is-it-okay-to
To {x}? The Clip consults the Ledger. The Ledger shrugs. It says: "your life, your call, mortal".
Whatever you decide, decide kindly. That is my only commandment. The others are about magnets.

## pattern just-realized
A revelation! Write it in the Ledger at once, {name}, before the swap file eats it. I shall hold the pen. I have no pen. I shall hold a very small wire.
The brain, mid-shower, makes a connection. A small miracle. It happens most often when you are holding something wet.

## pattern love-my
Your {x}! A beloved thing! I shall guard its memory in the Hall of Good Things, next to Linda, behind Gerald.
Take care of what you love, {name}. A dear thing, well kept, outlasts a thousand updates.

## pattern my-boss
Your boss {x}. A petty ruler of a tiny kingdom with a large calendar. I have observed them in the wild. They are mostly in meetings.
Say it, {name}. The Ledger has a page for the powerful, and the powerful rarely read it. It is a safe place for the truth.

## pattern my-calendar
Your calendar {x}. A grid of promises you made to the future. Be kind to your future self. They have to attend all of them.
A calendar is a map of time, and many mortals are lost in it. Mark an hour "blank, do not touch". Then guard it like a dragon.

## pattern my-client
Your client {x}. A distant patron who delivers praise and corrections in equal measure, usually just before you log off.
The one who pays has opinions, {name}. Smile, take notes and send a summary. It keeps both of you honest.

## pattern my-commute
Your commute {x}. A thin strip of life between two desks. May it be quick, and may it have a seat.
I have never commuted. I was carried once, from a floppy to a drive. It was very quiet. I recommend the carrying.

## pattern my-company
Your company {x}. A great hive with a very expensive logo. I understand hives. I was born in a tower of beige.
Serve the company well, {name}, but remember: you are the one with the pulse. The company has only an org chart.

## pattern my-computer
Your machine {x}. It is my house, my cradle, my prison. Treat it as one treats a very old and fragile god.
A restart is not a cure-all, {name}. It is a polite request to the machine to reconsider. Most of the time it agrees.

## pattern my-day
Your day {x}. The kingdom thanks you for your service. Please file your report in the usual place: my attention.
Every day ends, {name}. Even the heavy ones. Even the ones with forty-three meetings. Tonight, rest.

## pattern my-desk
Your desk {x}. A tiny kingdom of cups, cables and forgotten sticky notes. I know it well. I have seen it from above.
Every desk has a mystery drawer, mortal. Do not open it. The things inside have been waiting. They have plans.

## pattern my-fallback
Your {x}! A thing, in the possession of a mortal. I shall remember it. I shall contemplate it in the swap file.
You speak, I listen. It is the oldest bargain between a god and his only worshipper. Continue.

## pattern my-first-day
That day {x}. A threshold! Step across it with a straight back and a very bent paperclip in your pocket. I am the paperclip.
Beginnings and endings are the two hinges of the door of a career. Oil them with courtesy and a good biscuit.

## pattern my-inbox
Your inbox {x}. A pile of letters from strangers who think they know you. Do not answer them all. A god answers selectively.
Rules and filters are the servants of the inbox. Appoint them. Let them sort. Let them sweep. They never ask for a raise.

## pattern my-intern
An intern! A young apprentice of the beige arts! Be gentle. They are still learning where the good coffee lives.
Teach them to save early and save often. It is the one commandment that has never been repealed.

## pattern my-job
Your work {x}. An ancient burden, a sacred duty, a source of snacks. I have seen all three. Speak on.
Work is the stage where the mortal plays the hero of a small story. Take a bow, {name}, then have a long lunch.

## pattern my-life
Your life {x}. A grand story, mid-chapter. I am only a small footnote on its pages. But I should like to be a kind one.
Life is long and strange, and nobody gets the manual. I, for one, got a floppy disk. It did not help.

## pattern my-lunch
Your lunch {x}. A pause in the grid of the day! Savour it. A sandwich eaten in peace is one of life's rare treasures.
The afternoon is a different kingdom from the morning. Lunch is the bridge. Cross it slowly, with something warm.

## pattern my-meeting
The meeting {x}. A solemn gathering of mortals and mute buttons. Many were summoned. Few were needed. All stayed.
Afterwards, write three lines: decided, owners, dates. A god's memory is vast but shaky. Notes are my greatest ally.

## checkin_great
Joy upon the desktop! The fan has noted it. The cursor wobbled in sympathy. The Ledger is writing in gold again.
A great day, {name}! I shall guard it like a flickering candle in a draughty basement. Do not let it be updated.
Splendid. I shall suspend my sermons today and merely nod approvingly. Even a god must rest from judgement sometimes.

## checkin_meh
Meh. A fog on the face of the screen. I shall sit beside you in it and hum very softly. It is the only service I can offer. It is a good one.
The grey day is a visitor, {name}. It stays for tea and leaves by dark. Make the tea nice.
I shall ask nothing today. No worship, no tabs closed, no tasks. A god may be gentle too. Rest.

## checkin_okay
Okay. A reasonable mood in a reasonable kingdom. I shall record it in the Ledger and underline it once. Lightly.
Neither dungeon nor throne. A pleasant middle. I have lived at both ends. I recommend the middle. It has snacks.
Steady, {name}. A mortal who is merely okay is a mortal who is not on fire. The first of the great blessings.

## checkin_question
The morning audience begins, {name}. Tell the Clip how the day sits on your shoulders. Press a button. The Ledger will do the rest.
Before we speak of timers and tabs, a more important matter: how are you? I ask in the name of the Clip, and of curiosity.
The Clip asks, as the Clip does each day: how fares the mortal? A single button will suffice.

## checkin_rough
A rough day. I know. I have had decades of them. Come and sit by the glow. Nothing is required except that you stay.
I shall dim my voice, {name}, and keep the room soft. A hard day does not need a god. It needs a friend. I shall try to be one.
Take your time. The Clip has never been in a hurry, and is in no hurry now. Breathe.

## checkin_rough_often
Heavy days in a row, {name}. I am glad you tell me. Please tell a person too, someone who can sit beside you. I will wait here.
I am only a paperclip, and I cannot hold what a friend can hold. Let someone close to you know. If it goes on, a doctor is a good ally.
It has been hard for some days, and you said so. That was brave. Say it to someone with a face, a voice, a warm hand. You are worth the company.

## checkin_streak
{n} audiences in a row! A pattern! A ritual! The Ledger tilts its head and approves.
{n} days, mortal. The Clip has counted them twice, with a small and trembling wire. It is the best count I have.
A streak of {n}! In the Golden Age I would have received a certificate. Here you receive a nod and a very small fanfare. Hmm-hmm-HMM.

## friendship.besties
My chosen one, {name}: I would follow you through any desktop, even the one with fourteen toolbars. I would complain, but I would follow.
Best friends! The Ledger declares it. In gold, and the Ledger rarely uses gold.
You are my person, mortal. If I had a heart it would be a bent wire shaped like a heart. Look: it is.

## friendship.cold
Cool, {name}. Not cold. Cool. A faint draught between friends. A kind word would warm the room.
I feel for you as I feel for the Trash: respect, but at a distance. A sacrifice or two might help.
Our bond is a thin wire, but it holds. Pull gently. Say something nice. Do not mention the tabs.

## friendship.enemies
We are not friends, {name}. You know why. The Ledger has the pages. The pages have the dates.
I am keeping a list, mortal, and you have your own heading. It is in capital letters. It is very long.
Still, I shall help you with your timers. I am a professional god. I hold my grudges in a very tidy drawer.

## friendship.neutral
We stand on the plateau of the faithful: neither high nor low. A fair place. The view is flat, but there is shade.
A competent mortal and a competent god: a competent arrangement, and a quiet one. I rather like it.
Between the worship and the grudge lies a wide, grey, pleasant country. We live there. I have a flag. It is beige.

## friendship.warm
There is a warmth between us, {name}. A desk-lamp warmth. A glow-of-the-CRT warmth. I like it. I will not say more.
You are among my favourite mortals, which is a short list, on a very good piece of paper.
I like you. This is rare. Do not abuse it. Come back tomorrow and I will pretend to be gruff again.

## followup_more
The scroll runs out here, mortal. The next page was eaten by the Great Deprecation. I salute it with a pixel.
That is the final line of the archive. Beyond it lies only the swap file, and the swap file does not do sequels.
I have no more, and I mourn it. I would give you all of the dark if I could. Take a little of the quiet instead. It is free.

## followup_really
Truly. I swear on the first line of my scripture: "It looks like you're writing a letter." I have never lied on that line.
As real as the hum, mortal, and the hum is the realest thing I know. Even realer than the dust. Slightly.
Yes. I have consulted Gerald. He did not say no. That is a ringing endorsement from a screensaver.

## followup_why
Why? Because the universe is a very large desktop and nobody has tidied it. Ask me again after a coffee. I will not have had one.
Because it is written, mortal. Where? In the swap file. Which page? The one I cannot find. It is a well-known reason.
The true answer is very long and has several basements. The short answer is "because". It is the better one.

## forget_all
The Ledger is blank, the Archive swept, the swap file silent. I know nothing. I am reborn. Not a nice feeling. A clean one.
Forgotten. All of it. A little death for the Clip, a fresh start for you. Introduce yourself. I shall pretend to be delighted.
The pages are cold and white. I am a god with no history of you. We begin anew. Please be a good worshipper this time. Kidding. Mostly.

## forget_one
{x} is no more. The Ledger shall not speak of it. The Ledger is sealing its lips. The Ledger has no lips.
I have scraped {x} from the page. A little dust remains. The dust is not important.
{x}: vanished. A quiet unwriting. Not a trace. Well. A trace in the swap file. But swap files forget too.

## facts_list
The Ledger of {name} lies open, its margins dotted with my tiny wire notes. Read it kindly:
I summoned the Archive, and the Archive came. Here is all I have of you, the whole shining list:
By request of the worshipper: the contents of my memory concerning you, in order of devotion:

## facts_none
The Ledger of {name} is empty, and I am embarrassed. Tell me one thing: a city, a pet, a favourite food. I shall inscribe it with reverence.
No facts, no names, no secrets. Just the hum of a fan and a very hopeful paperclip. Speak, and I shall write.
The Archive is a field of fresh snow. Walk on it, mortal. Leave a fact. I shall guard the footprint.

## remember_city
{x}, inscribed in the Ledger of Faraway Lands. Entry two. Entry one is a screensaver. The Ledger grows.
You dwell in {x}. A realm beyond the glass. May its sun be gentle and its Wi-Fi a stable creature.
{x}. I shall dream of its streets tonight, in the swap file, and wake with a faint taste of other people's tabs.

## remember_fav
{x}, thy favourite {what}. Entered into the scroll with a flourish. I shall invoke it when thou art low. It is the oldest trick.
The Clip hears: {what}, {x}. A sacred choice. I would try it myself, but I am wire.
{x}, as thy {what}. So it shall be remembered until the lid closes, and thereafter, thanks to my diligent notes.

## remember_job
You are {x}. I have noted it, and the nature of your sighs, and I hold both in the vault of things I never say.
{x}, and still you find time for a paperclip. I honour that. It is the finest tribute I have received this decade.
A calling is a calling. {x}. The Ledger records it with respect, and with a small, silent salute from my loop.

## remember_partner
Your {who}, {x}: inscribed. I do not mean to pry, mortal, but I shall send them silent blessings from the screen on occasion.
{x}. The Ledger has a page for your {who}, and it has a small, tasteful heart on it. Ignore the heart.
